=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a string S of length N consisting of 0 and 1.
Move the K-th 1-block from the beginning in S to immediately after the (K-1)-th 1-block, and print the resulting string.
It is guaranteed that S contains at least K 1-blocks.
Here is a more precise description.

- Let S_{l\ldots r} denote the substring of S from the l-th character through the r-th character.
- We define a substring S_{l\ldots r} of S to be a 1-block if it satisfies all of the following conditions:
- S_l = S_{l+1} = \cdots = S_r =  1
- l = 1 or S_{l-1} =  0
- r = N or S_{r+1} =  0


- 
Suppose that all 1-blocks in S are S_{l_1\ldots r_1}, \ldots, S_{l_m\ldots r_m}, where l_1 < l_2 < \cdots < l_m.
Then, we define the length N string T, obtained by moving the K-th 1-block to immediately after the (K-1)-th 1-block, as follows:

- T_i = S_i for 1 \leq i \leq r_{K-1}
- T_i =  1 for r_{K-1} + 1 \leq i \leq r_{K-1} + (r_K - l_K) + 1
- T_i =  0 for r_{K-1} + (r_K - l_K) + 2 \leq i \leq r_K
- T_i = S_i for r_K + 1 \leq i \leq N

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N K
S

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 5 \times 10^5
- S is a string of length N consisting of 0 and 1.
- 2 \leq K
- S contains at least K 1-blocks.

Sample Input 1

15 3
010011100011001

Sample Output 1

010011111000001

S has four 1-blocks: from the 2nd to the 2nd character, from the 5th to the 7th character, from the 11th to the 12th character, and from the 15th to the 15th character.

Sample Input 2

10 2
1011111111

Sample Output 2

1111111110

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

We need find l_prev, r_prev, l_k, r_k. We can scan S and count blocks. Since K is known. We can identify block starts and ends. Need store previous block's start/end until K-th found. Approach:

block_count = 0
i = 0
l_prev = r_prev = l_k = r_k = -1
while i < N:
    if S[i] == '1':
        l = i
        while i < N and S[i] == '1':
            i += 1
        r = i - 1
        block_count += 1
        if block_count == K - 1:
            l_prev, r_prev = l, r
        elif block_count == K:
            l_k, r_k = l, r
            break
    else:
        i += 1

Then construct ans. Need ensure if K-1 block found, K block found. Since break after K, no need scan rest. Good. But there is subtlety: If block_count == K-1, store prev. Then continue scanning. When block_count increments to K, store K and break. Good.

Let's maybe implement a more compact scanning with no blocks list:

import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    K = int(data[1])
    S = data[2]
    cnt = 0
    i = 0
    l_prev = r_prev = l_k = r_k = -1
    while i < N:
        if S[i] == '1':
            l = i

                break

Let's maybe write final code:

import sys

def main():
    input_data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    N = int(input_data[0])
    K = int(input_data[1])
    S = input_data[2]

    cnt = 0
    i = 0
    r_prev = -1
    l_k = r_k = -1

    while i < N:
        if S[i] == '0':
            i += 1
            continue
        l = i
        while i < N and S[i] == '1':
            i += 1
        r = i - 1
        cnt += 1
        if cnt == K - 1:
            r_prev = r
        elif cnt == K:
            l_k = l
            r_k = r
            break

    ans = S[:r_prev + 1] + S[l_k:r_k + 1] + S[r_prev + 1:l_k] + S[r_k + 1:]
    sys.stdout.write(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

We don't need l_prev. Good. But wait, if we don't store l_prev, is there any issue? No. We only need r_prev, l_k, r_k. The prefix is up to r_prev. The gap is from r_prev+1 to l_k-1. Good.

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    N = int(data[0])
    K = int(data[1])
    S = data[2]

    cnt = 0
    i = 0
    r_prev = -1
    l_k = r_k = -1

    while i < N:
        if S[i] == '0':
            i += 1
            continue

        l = i
        while i < N and S[i] == '1':
            i += 1
        r = i - 1
        cnt += 1

        if cnt == K - 1:
            r_prev = r
        elif cnt == K:
            l_k = l
            r_k = r
            break

    ans = S[:r_prev + 1] + S[l_k:r_k + 1] + S[r_prev + 1:l_k] + S[r_k + 1:]
    sys.stdout.write(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
